Hyundai’s $359 IONIQ 5 lease looks like a deal, but don’t overlook its bigger sibling

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Hyundai IONIQ 5 is the most popular EV in America outside of Tesla, but its larger three-row sibling looks like the better deal right now.

Through September, the Hyundai IONIQ 5 was the third top-selling EV in the US, behind the Tesla Model Y and Model 3.

Hyundai’s electric SUV is outselling rivals like the Chevy Equinox EV, Ford Mustang Mach-E, and Toyota bZ.

Why? For one, it’s one of the most affordable EVs in the US, starting at about $35,000, and Hyundai has been offering some of the most attractive lease deals.

After the $7,500 federal EV tax credit expired at the end of September last year, Hyundai responded by reducing prices on the IONIQ 5 by up to nearly $10,000 on some trims.

The 2027 Hyundai IONIQ 5 starts at $35,250. That’s for the standard-range IONIQ 5 SE with an EPA-estimated range of up to 245 miles. Upgrading to the extended-range SE RWD model adds $2,500, starting at $37,750, and offers up to 318 miles of range.

Hyundai’s three-row electric SUV, the IONIQ 9, starts at $58,955, or nearly $24,000 more than the base IONIQ 5 SE.

On Hyundai’s website, the 2027 IONIQ 5 SE Standard Range is available to lease from $359 a month. The offer is for a 24-month lease with $4,999 due at signing for an effective cost of $567 per month.

The extended-range Hyundai IONIQ 5 SE RWD can be leased from $369 a month. The offer is also a 24-month lease with $4,999 due at signing, for an effective cost of $577 per month.

Hyundai’s larger IONIQ 9, meanwhile, is available to lease from $419 a month. That deal also includes $4,999 due at signing, but is for a 36-month lease, so the effective cost is $557 per month.

Using Hyundai’s payment calculator, it would cost about $412 a month to lease the 2027 Hyundai IONIQ 5 SE for 36 months with $4,999 due at signing. With an effective cost of $550 per month, it’s only $7 less than the three-row IONIQ 9.

The Hyundai IONIQ 9 offers more range than its smaller sibling, with an EPA-estimated range of up to 335 miles for the base SE trim.

The IONIQ 5 is a midsize electric SUV, about the same size as a Tesla Model Y, with two rows and enough room for five passengers. Hyundai’s larger IONIQ 9 offers three rows of seating and six- or seven-seat configurations.

With an added row of seating, the IONIQ 9 offers up to 163.54 cubic feet of interior passenger volume, compared with the IONIQ 5, which offers up to 106.5 cubic feet.

While the IONIQ 9 may be the better deal right now, the IONIQ 5 is still a good fit for those looking for a smaller, more efficient SUV that doesn’t necessarily need the added space.

In comparison, the standard Tesla Model Y RWD is listed for lease starting at $499 a month with an EPA range of up to 321 miles. That’s for a 36-month lease with $3,000 down.

Using Tesla’s payment calculator, if you put the same $4,999 down for a 24-month lease like the IONIQ 5, the Model Y RWD’s monthly rate is $549.

Which one would you choose? The IONIQ 5, IONIQ 9, or Tesla Model Y? Let us know why in the comments.

Both the IONIQ 5 and IONIQ 9 are built at Hyundai’s Metaplant America outside of Savannah, Georgia.

I really haven’t seen much value in leasing. I’d rather buy the car outright using cash, with the goal of keeping the car for 10+ years.

My family recently bought a Hyundai IONIQ 5, and it is an outstanding car. Loaded with great features — both on the EV side and then for safety and comfort.

When we toured the facility earlier this year, Hyundai told us that its electric SUVs are attracting new buyers to the brand at a higher rate than its gas or hybrid vehicles.

The IONIQ 5 had a 69.8% conquest rate last year, while the IONIQ 9 had 64.3%. Hyundai’s Elantra N ranked third (61.9%), followed by the Palisade (61.2%) and Kona Electric (60.9%).

Hyundai’s current offers end on November 30, 2026. Want to see what’s available near you? You can use the links below to find available Hyundai IONIQ 5 and IONIQ 9 models near you, along with any current offers.

So...What’s Going on With the Bachelor Franchise? We Finally Have a Premiere Date for ‘The Bachelor’ Season 30

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Ever since ABC pulled the plug on Taylor Frankie Paul’s season of The Bachelorette just days before its premiere, fans have been wondering: What’s next for the Bachelor franchise? And after a month of rumors surrounding the fate of The Bachelor and its many spin-offs, fans finally have an actual update. According to Variety, The Bachelor season 30 is set to premiere on Sunday, March 21, 2027.

It was first revealed earlier this year by Deadline that both The Bachelor and Bachelor in Paradise would return for new seasons in 2027.

“The Bachelor is almost a perfect format and it is really powerful,” Disney’s reality chief Rob Mills told the outlet at the time. “It’s over two decades old, and it’s seen really good days and really bad days. But it’s always had a lot of resilience, especially when the people making it really, really care about it, because that fan base also really cares about it. However The Bachelor comes back next year, it will be done with a lot of thoughtfulness and care. I do think better days are ahead for the franchise.”

As a reminder, season 22 of The Bachelorette was scheduled to premiere on March 22 but was canceled amid reports that Taylor and her ex, Dakota Mortensen, were involved in a domestic dispute.

A statement from a Disney Entertainment Television spokesperson was released on March 19, saying, “In light of the newly released video just surfaced today, we have made the decision to not move forward with the new season of The Bachelorette at this time, and our focus is on supporting the family.”

Rumors about how the franchise would move forward (if at all) have been circulating ever since, as the decision to cancel Taylor’s season reportedly also impacted production on The Bachelor and Bachelor in Paradise. TMZ previously reported that the network had planned to cast one of the finalists on Taylor’s season as the next Bachelor but “it was all predicated on that contender becoming famous” from Taylor’s season…which, of course, didn’t happen.

Likewise, a separate source told TMZ that the season 22 contestants would possibly have another chance at love on the upcoming season of Bachelor in Paradise, though “it’s unclear how many might get that opportunity.”

And according to yet another source for People, Warner Bros. reportedly held a meeting with Taylor’s men on the same day the show was canceled (save for her winner, Doug Mason) and told them their “contracts will be in place for a year.”

As for Taylor’s unaired season of The Bachelorette? Rob told THR that he doesn’t know whether it will ever air on ABC or stream on Hulu and is taking everything “a day at a time”...but at this point, it feels safe to conclude that season 22 of The Bachelorette will be shelved indefinitely.

10 Fall Dinners With 10 Ingredients (Or Fewer!)

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Fall means the start of the school year and holiday after holiday, which means busy mornings and even busier days (and chillier too!). If you feel like you never have the time to run to the grocery store and pick up tons of ingredients for endless weeknight dinners, we feel you. And with grocery prices still climbing, the last thing you want is a recipe that requires a long shopping list but still leaves you wanting more.

These 10 fall dinners check all the boxes. They're some of the easiest and quickest weeknight meals you can make, and best of all, each one requires no more than 10 ingredients, so you won't have to stock up on a bunch of extras.

The best weeknight dinners use pantry powerhouse ingredients to bring big flavor in a short time, and these creamy harissa chickpeas are a prime example. Harissa, tomato paste, and coconut milk join forces with chickpeas to create a saucy one-pan dinner that’s as quick to make as it is wildly craveable.

These cranberry brie turkey melts are precisely what they call for: rustic slices of artisan bread, tangy cranberry sauce, creamy buttery brie, and a mound of garlicky leftover turkey.

Not all sheet-pan dinners are created equal. Sheet-pan pork chops with apples falls into the camp of excellence, packing in flavor with a short-ingredient list onto one pan, all of which comes together in just over a half an hour.

Tossed in a fresh garlic-burst cherry tomato sauce and served with slabs of bubbling browned feta, these chickpeas are your weeknight ticket to protein-packed dinnertime bliss.

With the addition of one of fall’s flavor superstars—pumpkin, of course!—spaghetti carbonara gets a seasonal twist that'll have you craving it all season long. Adding pumpkin puree gives a creamy, silky, and subtly sweet boost to the otherwise simple sauce.

In this easy, hands-off version, we’re cooking thinly-sliced steak low and slow in a rich broth until it’s fall-apart delicious, and then throwing in the broccoli near the end so it cooks just long enough to become tender. After that, all you need is a pot of rice, and dinner is served!

Don't let chicken breasts have all the fun! Chicken thighs are super-flavorful, easy to cook, and SO delicious with sweet potatoes and apples. Try it for dinner any night this fall.

We’re big fans of one-pot pastas and French onion soup-flavored everything, so it was just a matter of time until we combined those two awesome things in one dish. Meet this one-pot French onion soup pasta, which is destined to become one of your favorite weeknight dinners.

Our creamy Tuscan chicken has consistently been one of our most-popular recipes since we published it, which has inspired our test kitchen to create countless iterations. Practically anything is delicious swimming in this creamy Tuscan-inspired sauce, but this vegetarian white bean dinner is our favorite.

This is a true 30-minute meal, and it calls for ingredients that you might already have on hand, saving you a trip to the grocery store. This saucy one-pan dinner features a base of fresh garlic, sun-dried tomatoes, thyme, heavy cream, and Parmesan tossed with pillowy-soft and quick-cooking gnocchi.

In Some Ways, Bluetti’s Step-Down Apex 200 is a Step Up From the Apex 300

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

This power station is one of Bluetti’s most multifunctional yet and a boon to solar power lovers.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

When I reviewed the Bluetti Apex 300 power station earlier this year, I was impressed with how powerful it was and how easy it was to expand into a robust home backup system. But there were a few notable trade-offs, mostly in portability and a lower variety of outlets. With Bluetti’s Apex 200 power station, those drawbacks are answered with a powerful model that’s much better suited to life on the road.

Physically, it’s not hard to notice the difference between the two units. At 84 pounds, the Apex 300 is large and heavy to tote around. The 200 is 59 pounds and roughly 28 percent smaller. It’s not as powerful as its larger sibling, but the 200 is still definitely built for use in the home, while being more adaptable for long road trips or cross-country treks in an RV.

The Apex 200 outputs up to 3,200 watts of continuous power (it says 3,600 on the front, but that’s only if it’s connected to an expansion battery). That’s enough to run several appliances or even a few circuits in your home electrical system if you connect it to a manual transfer switch using the 120-volt, 30-amp port. It also has a 2,560-watt-hour capacity, which can provide several hours of power—I ran my refrigerator for a full 27 hours on a single charge, and when I ran the numbers, I estimated that it runs just over 90 percent efficiently, which is very good for a power station.

The 200 also brings direct DC power back, with a bank of USB outlets and both 10- and 30-amp 12-volt outputs. This is something the Apex 300 doesn’t have built-in (it requires an optional hub to add it), which makes it a bit less versatile as a portable station.

One niche feature I really liked was the ability to turn off each AC outlet individually using the mobile app. Doing this helps to cut down on the slight battery drain that an idle outlet can contribute, and it’s not commonly seen on power stations. It might seem like a small thing, but when you’re trying to get the most runtime out of your power station, every little bit helps. It’s also handy if you want to turn off power to one device, but not another.

What you don’t get with the Apex 200, however, is 240-volt output for higher-powered applications like powering high-voltage appliances or HVAC systems. That means it’s not suited for advanced whole-home backup like the Apex 300 is, but it's fine if you're only powering 120-volt appliances.

The Apex 200 does, however, mimic the modularity of the 300. You can add up to six additional batteries to expand your capacity to over 33 kilowatt-hours using the largest batteries Bluetti offers, the B500K. You can also pair it with the Apex 300 to combine and increase total power output to over 7,000 watts.

One of the most surprising features I found with the Apex 200 was a maximum solar input of up to 3,000 watts. That’s incredibly high for a power station in this size range. It achieves this by offering two sets of inputs: low-voltage XT60 input (up to 1,000 watts) and high-voltage MC4 input (up to 2,000 watts). If you had the rated panels to support both of these inputs, you could fully recharge this station in just a few hours on a perfectly sunny day.

This attention to detail on solar recharging makes the Apex 200 one of the most robust power stations I’ve seen targeted to the on-the-go end user who likes to tap into the sun's rays for energy while off-grid and on the road. Of course, it also features standard charging from AC power, including a Turbo mode that supports fast charging, an auto mode that allows you to set the rate you prefer, or a silent mode that limits the amount of input so that the cooling fan doesn’t need to turn on (which on this model can be a bit loud).

Another feature I like is passthrough charging, which allows you to run devices from the station while recharging the battery at the full rate. The inverters in lesser power stations will throttle the input if they need to power something at the same time, but not here. This is true of the Apex 300, too, and I’m glad they kept this feature in the Apex 300.

For my money, this power station is best suited to those who plan to use the Apex 200 for limited home backup during emergencies to power 120-volt devices or circuits, while also wanting to take it on trips. Overlanders, especially, will appreciate the lighter weight and portability, and they’ll also love the high solar input, which can come in handy when you’re on the road and out of range of AC power. I still like the Apex 300 a lot, but the Apex 200 shows that a bit more versatility can go a long way.

David is a Reviews Editor for Hearst’s Enthusiast Group, contributing to Popular Mechanics, Runner’s World, Bicycling, Best Products, and Biography. He has over 25 years of experience writing about and reviewing consumer electronics, kitchen appliances, DIY products, outdoor gear, and more. He also spent years covering the ever-changing world of online retail. To relax, he loves experimenting with new recipes, tweaking his smart home setup, and spending time outdoors on his bike or camping.

You can mix a wild cocktail of textures and colors, play recklessly with silhouettes and experiment to your heart’s content—and your wallet’s demise. But no doubt the best part of dressing for the best season is the outerwear.

And right now, the shelves are stocked with sick pieces, which means my tabs are so more packed than a Tokyo train during rush hour. From dramatic overcoats to slick truckers, artisanal macs and swishy windbreakers, the menswear gods are really smiling upon us right now. Luis Enrique: PSG focused on the present after ‘bizzare’ win at Villarreal

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Luis Enrique insisted he would not be looking too deep into Paris Saint-Germain's thumping win over Villarreal in the Champions League, ahead of facing Troyes on Saturday.

PSG made Champions League history with their 6-0 victory at the Estadio de la Ceramica on Tuesday, blowing their opponents away with a flurry of goals in the first half.

The Parisiens led 4-0 after just 17 minutes against Villarreal, the second-earliest a team has scored four in a European Cup or Champions League match, after Benfica against Ujpest in November 1960 (16th minute).

PSG also became the first team in Champions League history to score 4+ goals in four consecutive games in the competition, while they have now reeled off 10 consecutive wins in all competitions for the second time under Luis Enrique (also 10 between January and March 2025).

Luis Enrique's side also took their goals total in the Champions League to 20 after their opening four matches, the fewest games any team has taken to reach that total.

"It was an unusual match with four goals in eight minutes, which isn't normal. It's almost bizarre, difficult to play," Luis Enrique said of the win. "I don't like this kind of game.

"I much prefer to win rather than lose, but normally there's not that sort of difference between the teams. I think it was the same thing between Lens and Como.

"It's an accident, it can happen to any team. It can happen in football, but it's not the norm. Villarreal is in the past, Troyes is another match.

"There's a lot of expectation and that's why I'm concerned."

Matchday eve at the PSG Campus! https://t.co/HfSv05wbcj

— Paris Saint-Germain (@PSG_English) November 6, 2026

The only negative from PSG's midweek win was an injury to Maghnes Akliouche, who the club have confirmed will require treatment over the coming days.

PSG also released a statement saying an agreement had been reached that Achraf Hakimi would have a period of rest ahead of the upcoming international break.

Hakimi was key to PSG's 5-1 win over Le Havre in their last league game. He was involved in all five of PSG's goals (three goals, two assists), as many contributions as he had in his previous 32 appearances combined (two goals, three assists).

Troyes are languishing at the opposite end of the table, sitting 16th in Ligue 1 despite going unbeaten in their last two matches in the competition.

"We know there's a lot of expectation against Troyes. Everyone expects us to score a lot of goals, but that's not football," Luis Enrique added on the task of facing Troyes.

"We have to stay focused on this game. If we're thinking about Villarreal and start Saturday's game distracted, you can play badly.

"But it's always with the same intensity. It's like that you can achieve new things by staying focused on the present.

"If we win on Saturday, it'll be the first time that we have won all our games between the two international windows. That's a source of additional motivation."

Having scored in his last two Ligue 1 appearances, Mayulu could become the third-youngest player to score in three consecutive top-flight games for the club at 20 years and 174 days old, after Kylian Mbappe (18y 361d) and Desire Doue (19y 320d).

Mayulu also found the back of the net in their thumping win over Villarreal, and he will want to keep that run going at the Parc des Princes.

— Paris Saint-Germain (@PSG_English) November 3, 2026

Troyes are one of three teams in Europe's top five leagues, alongside Lorient and Schalke, not to have a single player with more than one goal this season.

Mille is one of those players to find the back of the net, while he has also created more chances (15) than any other player for Troyes in Ligue 1 so far.

PSG have won nine of their 11 home Ligue 1 matches against Troyes (D2), only hosting Strasbourg (35 times) and Bastia (29) more often without ever losing in the competition.

Luis Enrique's side have also scored in each of their last 29 Ligue 1 matches (68 goals in total) and have only had a longer run once, which was 44 games under Thomas Tuchel between August 2018 and September 2019.

The Parisiens have also netted in each of their last 53 home games in Ligue 1 (134 goals in total, 2.5 per match), with no team having a longer such run since Nice between 1975 and 1978 (55).

Troyes, however, are unbeaten in their last two Ligue 1 matches (W1 D1) but have not had a longer run in the same season since between August and September 2022 (three). They could keep a third consecutive clean sheet in Ligue 1 for the first time since May 2013 (three).

Troyes have avoided defeat in just two of their 11 away Ligue 1 games against the reigning champions (D2), but they did score three goals in their most recent such match, a 4-3 loss against PSG in October 2022.

Troyes have failed to score in each of their last three away games (0-2 v Lille, 0-2 v Angers, 0-0 v Toulouse), as many times as in their previous 17 in all competitions. They could fail to score in four consecutive away Ligue 1 matches for the first time since between November and December 2012 (four).

Pablo Helguera to Helm the New School’s Vera List Center

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Upon his appointment, the artist reflected on the urgency he feels to “make sense of rapidly unfolding events, question prevailing narratives, and imagine alternative futures.”

Artist, educator, and writer Pablo Helguera has been appointed as the new director of the New School's Vera List Center for Art and Politics (VLC), succeeding Senior Director and Chief Curator Carin Kuoni, who steered the arts-driven research center for 22 years. Helguera was also tapped as the director of the university's art collection, which consists of over 2,000 postwar and contemporary works.

Having entered the new role this Monday, November 2, Helguera takes the reins after six years of serving as an assistant professor and director of the Arts Management and Entrepreneurship graduate program at the New School's College of Performing Arts. Helguera was previously the director of adult and academic programs at the Museum of Modern Art for over a decade.

As an artist, Helguera centers social engagement through public performance and installation, archival findings, pedagogical practices, and traditional media. Among his most famous projects is “The School of Panamerican Unrest” (2006), a cross-continental road trip from Anchorage, Alaska, to Ushuaia, capital of Argentina's southernmost Tierra del Fuego province, that culminated in over 100 public engagement events across 25 cities in 20 countries. Helguera is also known for developing Librería Donceles, a traveling used bookstore with thousands of Spanish-language texts that visited 13 countries.

The social, ideological, and cultural frameworks of language are prominent themes that Helguera explores in his practice, which coalesces with his roles in education and public programming. He became a Guggenheim Fellow in 2008, and additional recognitions include support from the Franklin Furnace Archive, Art Matters, and Blade of Grass; a Creative Capital grant; and an honorary doctorate from the Kansas City Art Institute.

Te Araroa: several zeros in a row

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

When you’ve centred your whole next several months on walking it becomes really hard to stop. I knew I needed some real rest days to get my ankle back to a functional state, and I needed to wait for a physio appointment. But, man, do you ever start to go crazy when you just stop. I dried my tent (it was spread out for DAYS), scrubbed as much Raetea mud off my gear as I possibly could, washed my pot and cup and all my bottles, I back-flushed my filter and did laundry, made a plan for the next sections and resupplies, caught up with my day-one mates and then… nothing, there was nothing else to do.

The perch where I iced my ankle for four days

Unfortunately after one and a half rest days my ankle was still stiff and sore, but every day it improved slightly.

I spent my unexpected zeroes with the absolute kindest trail angels who let me stay in a self-contained house on their property, gave me eggs and beers and fried up some chorizo to add to my leftover pizza. It was not all bad. In fact, it would have been sort of luxurious if not for the stress of trying to heal an injury. I watched all the nostalgic DVDs (looking at you Amanda Bynes movies) in the house and ate as much fresh produce as I could. I tried to enjoy myself but five days is like 100 when all you want is to be out there walking.

The next section was going to be easier, lots more road walking but less intense terrain. I was looking forward to being around hikers again.

I made my way to Paihia via the Waitangi treaty grounds. This is where the country of New Zealand was essentially born (though it’s much more complicated than that). I had been there years ago but missed the museum portion so wanted to take this opportunity to visit it. It was great! Especially as someone whose doctorate is in museum studies.

With the all-clear from the physio and armed with a fresh roll of k tape and a jar of tiger balm I started walking again. Two short days (about 16km each) to warm me back up into walking. I’m so happy to be back on trail!

I'm Lindsay, a Canadian who moved to NZ almost eight years ago to do a PhD, I fell in love with NZ and never left. Now I spend my time convincing my friends to do big walks with me and taking on overly-ambitious craft projects. This season I'm attempting a thru-hike of Te Araroa, starting October 2026.

Report - It's happening, star waiting on green light for Liverpool return

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

One star could soon return to Liverpool. He's waiting on the green light and we fully expect him to get it.

Remember Jürgen Klopp's kids? Few of them actually remain at the club, even if that was under three years ago. Liverpool instead decided to cash in on them, using the money to finance an enormous spend over the last two summers.

It's a strategy we don't really agree with. Liverpool don't seem better off for it, while they're now up against the homegrown quota and need to find way to manoeuvre around it.

But that isn't to say that they cut ties with all of those players. Some they absolutely didn't - everyone is aware at this point that Jarell Quansah has a buyback clause, for instance, and could return to the club next summer.

We'll have to wait and see if Liverpool activate that clause but before then, another one of that generation could return to Anfield. We're talking as soon as the winter window.

Harvey Elliott went out on loan to Valencia this summer, following on from a disastrous spell at Aston Villa last season. That spell really couldn't have gone worse - Villa were so eager not to trigger a buy clause that they simply didn't play Elliott for a year.

It felt, honestly, like Liverpool had failed him as much as Villa had. They could have brought him back in the January to find a different solution but refused to. Instead, Elliott lost a year of his career, effectively.

There was more hope for the Valencia loan, however, that was misplaced. Elliott is struggling to find minutes there and hasn't impressed with his performances. Maybe that's not a massive surprise after losing a season last term.

Plaza Deportiva now report that Elliott could be sent back to Liverpool in January. Valencia increasingly believe they should just call it quits as the player is struggling to adapt there.

And if this is the case, Liverpool should absolutely end the loan early. It makes no real sense to leave Elliott on loan again if he isn't wanted - even financially, it just tanks his valuation and benefits no one.

But morally, too, this has all been handled so poorly that forcing him to struggle on a loan that ultimately leads to nothing positive is awful after last season.

Planning and taking a trip can be exciting, but unexpected events can disrupt even the most meticulously organized vacations. Sudden illness, severe weather and unforeseen circumstances can throw a wrench in the works, forcing you to change your plans and seek compensation.

Fortunately, many credit cards include trip cancellation and interruption insurance at no additional cost when you use your card to book travel. The right card can reimburse nonrefundable expenses if your plans fall apart.

Here are the best credit cards for trip cancellation and interruption insurance — and how their coverage compares.

*Eligibility and benefit level varies by card. Terms, conditions and limitations apply. Please visit americanexpress.com/benefitsguide for more details. Underwritten by New Hampshire Insurance Company, an AIG Company.

The cards listed above offer some of the strongest trip cancellation and interruption protections available. However, many other travel credit cards also include some form of coverage — often with lower limits.

Related: Credit cards with travel insurance

Trip cancellation and trip interruption insurance can reimburse prepaid, nonrefundable travel expenses if you cancel or alter your trip for a covered reason. Coverage limits, eligible expenses and qualifying events vary by card.

In general, trip cancellation insurance applies when you must cancel before departure, and trip interruption insurance applies if your trip is cut short after it begins.

Unlike travel insurance policies with optional “cancel for any reason” coverage, credit card coverage typically applies only to specific covered events outlined in your card’s guide to benefits. Covered reasons often include illness, injury or severe weather, but exclusions apply.

Because benefits can change, always review your card’s guide before booking travel.

Related: Should you get travel insurance if you have credit card protection?

Chase credit cards offer some of the most comprehensive trip cancellation and interruption protections among major issuers.

The and the each provide coverage of up to $10,000 per covered person and $20,000 per trip. Despite their different annual fees, $95 and $795, respectively, both cards offer top-tier protection limits.

The , United's highest-tier personal card and the one best suited for frequent United travelers, also provides coverage of up to $10,000 per covered person and $20,000 per trip, per the card's Guide to Benefits.

You and your immediate family members may be eligible for coverage when you pay for all or a portion of your trip with an eligible Chase card or redeem rewards associated with that account, including through Chase Travel℠.

Other Chase cards offer lower limits. For example, the (see rates and fees), the (see rates and fees) and the (see rates and fees) typically provide coverage of up to $5,000 per covered person and $10,000 per trip.

Meanwhile, cards such as the (see rates and fees), the (see rates and fees) and the (see rates and fees) also offer trip cancellation and interruption insurance but with lower coverage limits. These cards typically reimburse only nonrefundable common carrier fares and offer significantly smaller per-person caps than the Sapphire cards.

Coverage generally applies only to the portion of the trip charged to your eligible card (or booked using associated rewards).

For example, if you pay for a nonrefundable hotel stay with gift cards but charge your flight to your Chase card, you likely won’t be reimbursed for the hotel if you cancel — even if the flight was covered.

Always consult your card’s guide to benefits for full terms, conditions and exclusions.

Related: Your guide to Chase’s trip insurance coverage

Select American Express cards offer trip cancellation and trip interruption insurance. Each benefit provides coverage of up to $10,000 per covered trip, with a separate maximum of $20,000 per eligible card per 12 consecutive months.

*Eligibility and benefit level varies by card. Terms, conditions and limitations apply. Please visit americanexpress.com/benefitsguide for more details. Underwritten by New Hampshire Insurance Company, an AIG Company.

Unlike many issuers, eligible American Express cards may also cover traveling companions, not just immediate family members. A traveling companion is defined as someone who has made advance arrangements to travel with you or your family members for all or part of a covered trip.

To qualify, you must charge the full cost of round-trip common carrier transportation to an eligible Amex card or use a combination of your card and Membership Rewards points, vouchers or loyalty program certificates.

You can find benefit guides for eligible cards on Amex’s website.

Related: Your complete guide to travel protections on American Express cards

Who is covered by credit card trip cancellation and interruption insurance varies greatly. For example, eligible premium Chase cards cover the cardholder and immediate family members when the cardholder pays for all or a portion of a trip with their card account or rewards associated with their account.

On the other hand, eligible American Express cards cover the cardholder, their family members and traveling companions. The guide to benefits for eligible Amex cards defines a traveling companion as “an individual who has made advanced arrangements with you or your Family Members to travel together for all or part of a Covered Trip.”

Definitions of “family member” and “traveling companion” differ, and benefits are subject to change. Review your guide to benefits to confirm eligibility before booking.

Related: Are authorized users covered by Chase’s credit card travel insurance?

A benefits administrator can usually walk you through specific steps to make your claim. Luckily, many card issuers also allow you to make claims online, so you may not even need to hop on the phone to send any physical documents.

Related: Who is covered by credit card travel insurance?

Generally, trip cancellation and trip interruption insurance will cover nonrefundable expenses if you need to cancel your trip (or the remainder of your trip) due to a natural disaster. However, credit card insurance policies vary, so check your benefits guide for specific exclusions.

Note that you must book your trip before the natural disaster starts to be covered. If a storm or hurricane is named and you book your trip afterward, you likely won’t be covered.

Related: 4 times your credit card’s travel insurance can help with travel woes, and 7 times it won’t

Trip cancellation and interruption insurance can be one of the most valuable — and overlooked — benefits on a travel credit card.

Coverage details vary widely by issuer and by card, so it’s important to review your guide to benefits before booking.

For expensive trips, you may still consider stand-alone travel insurance, but the right credit card can provide meaningful protection without paying extra.

Related: Flight canceled or delayed? Here’s what to do next

Your Houseplants Need a New Care Routine for Winter—Here Are 4 Easy Habits to Start

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Give your indoor greenery a fighting chance until spring.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

With colder weather drawing our attention indoors, many of us swap outdoor garden maintenance for caring for our favorite houseplants. But while your spider plant, peace lily, or monstera may be safely tucked away inside, their needs change significantly during autumn and winter.

Reduced daylight, cooler temperatures, and drier indoor air can all affect the health of houseplants, meaning the care routine that worked perfectly in summer could actually do more harm than good during the colder months.

It turns out many of us are unknowingly making a few common mistakes that can leave indoor plants struggling through winter. Here are the habits to avoid, plus the simple swaps that will help keep your houseplants healthy until spring.

You might assume that moving your houseplant closer to a bright window is an excellent idea in winter to allow it to drink in as much sunlight as possible. And while this isn't necessarily a bad move, it just requires a little more thought.

“It’s always a good idea to move them closer to a bright window as darker days hit, but make sure to avoid cold drafts and radiators,” Mark Sage, B&Q's horticulture expert, tells us. “Cold drafts and dry air from radiators can all take their toll on indoor plants, leading to yellowing leaves, rotten roots or even plant fatality.”

Similarly, Sage suggests thinking about where the sun actually shines in your home throughout autumn and winter.

“Where natural light may be limited, consider investing in a plant light to give an extra boost, while rotating larger plants daily can help avoid leggy, awkward growth as they stretch out looking for the sunshine,” he suggests.

When houseplants start to look a little worse for wear and growth begins to slow over winter, it's tempting to step up your care routine. You might find yourself watering more often, tending to leaves more regularly, or even feeding plants in an attempt to encourage new growth.

However, Sage warns that this is often the wrong approach. In fact, during autumn and winter, “sometimes the best thing you can do for your plants is leave them alone!”

He explains: “In a slower growth period, plants generally need less water and feeding, and winter certainly isn’t the time for regular repotting.”

Instead, Sage recommends letting the soil dry out between waterings to help prevent root rot and giving plants time to rest. “It's worth giving them a boost of plant feed ahead of the winter months, but a little goes a long way,” he says. “Give plants their final dose at the end of autumn.”

During summer, when houseplants are actively growing, it's common to spread them throughout your home so you can enjoy a touch of greenery in different rooms. However, according to Sage, this can work against them once temperatures drop.

Instead, he recommends bringing your plants together during autumn and winter to help recreate the conditions many tropical houseplants naturally prefer.

“Give your houseplants a helping hand through winter by clustering them together to create a more humid microclimate, as many of these plants originally come from much hotter parts of the world,” he recommends. “Grouping several varieties close together can help maintain humidity around them, which can be particularly useful when central heating makes indoor air drier.”

While watering may have been your main plant-care task during summer, it's a mistake to neglect cleaning your houseplants over autumn and winter. Just like the rest of your home, plants can gather dust, and that build-up can make it harder for them to make the most of limited daylight.

“During winter, leaves can gather a layer of dust,” says Sage. “Give them a wipe down every one to two weeks with a microfibre cloth to support photosynthesis, and to help them benefit from even the smallest burst of sunlight.”

In fact, Sage suggests combining cleaning and watering occasionally by giving your plants a gentle shower.

“Use lukewarm water to boost humidity and get rid of any pests, but keep an eye on the temperature as if it is too cold you can shock the roots, but too warm and you can scald the leaves.”

Every Pokémon Gimmick, Ranked

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Aden Carter is a Contributor at DualShockers who has been covering games professionally since 2020. He specializes in lists and guides, with a focus on RPGs, horror games, survival horror, Soulslikes, cozy/sim games, and action-adventure titles. Before joining DualShockers, Aden contributed to GameRant, XDA, Gamepur, I

Over the years, we have seen many different Pokémon gimmicks pop up. It seems like every generation of Pokémon needs to have its own gimmick to make it stand out from the others.

It has come to a point where many fans of the series are tired of seeing new gimmicks pop up, and they would rather Pokémon go back to its roots with simple turn-based battles.

Unfortunately, that is never going to be the case, so you must accept whatever gimmick is in each game. Some of these gimmicks aren’t bad, but other ones are rather questionable. Let’s compare each of these gimmicks and see how they stack up against each other.

After introducing Mega Evolutions to Pokémon, Game Freak needed something to top it. Unfortunately, the gimmick in the next games, Sun and Moon, ended up being what is considered the worst gimmick in the Pokémon series.

The gimmick in question is Z-Moves. Z-Moves allow your Pokémon to use a massively powerful move, provided they are holding the appropriate Z-Crystal. Many have said that requiring an item to use one all-powerful move is a bit lame.

These all-powerful moves are often viewed as the one-hit option, allowing you to clear battles easily without much strategy being involved. The gimmick often felt unbalanced, and the addition of annoying, unskippable cutscenes, made using the moves extremely annoying.

I am someone who absolutely loves the Paldea region. I think that it is a beautiful area, and the idea that battles are celebrated in massive arenas is actually really cool to me. However, this generation of Pokémon games has one of the worst gimmicks, and that is Dynamaxing.

Dynamaxing allows a Pokémon to grow massive, amplifying its power in the process, and giving it access to different moves. In theory, it sounds decent. However, it is a nightmare for competitive play, as it makes already extremely powerful Pokémon even more powerful instead of lifting up and helping weaker Pokémon.

In addition to this, the moves given to the Pokémon are not unique, as they become different moves based on the previous move’s type, so a flying-type move will always become the same move when Dynamaxed. There are even more issues with it, but it all boils down to the gimmick feeling like not much thought went into it, as it is rather unbalanced and makes battles a bit of a slog.

Our picks for the best Pokémon Champions teams that are sure to help you climb this season.

If you were around for the Ruby and Sapphire days of Pokémon, then you remember double battles. These changed the way that Pokémon battles were played, and then they were expanded on in a rather weird way when triple Battles and rotational Battles appeared in Pokémon Black and White.

These battle types have rarely appeared since their inception, and it is because they weren’t very popular at the time. Triple battles are simple, involving three Pokémon being on the field at once instead of two. This is a bit unnecessary, as double battles were already fine.

Additionally, rotational battles were annoying because you had the ability to rotate your Pokémon around whenever you wanted, making some moves completely obsolete. However, it did make things interesting because the mechanic of rotating your Pokémon was different from switching them in and out of battle.

Terastallization is a gimmick that has the fanbase rather mixed, as there are many who enjoy the mechanic, and then there are those who absolutely hate it. As far as things go, it is a rather middle-of-the-road gimmick that could have been better, but it isn’t that bad.

This gimmick, which was introduced in Pokémon Scarlet and Violet, allows you to crystalize your Pokémon, changing its typing in the process. Many enjoy the mechanic because it lets you change the Pokémon’s typing, making them more resistant to moves they were once weak against.

The argument against Terastallization is that it can be unbalanced in certain battle formats, but it is mostly a balanced mechanic that works well with the series. The other argument is that every Pokémon looks absolutely terrible when it Terastalizes, and to be honest, they aren’t wrong.

Whether they're unlikely or just silly, these are the Ridiculous Pokémon Mega Evolutions We Would Love To See.

As stated earlier in the article, double battles were introduced in Pokémon Ruby and Sapphire, and they have remained to this day. It is one of the few earlier gameplay gimmicks that people sometimes forget started as a gimmick because it was so beloved.

Never before had players thrown two Pokémon into battle at once, and it felt like a natural progression of the battle system, allowing players to have two Pokémon whose types complimented each other.

Plus, this generation introduced new moves that were meant to be used during double battles. This gimmick has not changed since its inception, and it is because it feels natural for the series. There is nothing that needs to be changed about it, which makes it one of the best Game Freak has added to the series.

Why is it that I always forget that Mega Evolution is something that started in Pokémon X and Y? I always think that it was the gimmick from Pokémon Black and White. Tangent aside, Mega Evolution has become the most popular gimmick that Game Freak has added to the games.

It is so popular that people have continuously asked for more and more Pokémon to receive Mega Evolutions because the transformations are not only cool, but they are meaningful.

When Mega Evolution happens, it can sometimes change a Pokémon’s type, it can change the Pokémon’s ability, and it boosts the Pokémon’s stats tremendously. This can change the tide of a battle, but it isn’t so powerful that it is the ultimate trump card. It doesn’t allow you to blow away the opposition, but rather gives you more of a fighting chance and can dramatically help in certain situations. To this day, there are still Mega Evolutions being created, and it has been the focus in some competitive games as well.

I've missed Pokemon's Mega Evolution mechanic, and it's going to be a huge draw for Pokemon Legends: Z-A players.

Share your take with fellow players. Keep it constructive, relevant, and respectful.

Susie Wiles Is Out. What Will Trump Do When He Runs Out of Women to Fire?

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

You know how after millions of people make clear that they despise not just your political project but also you personally, the logical response is to triple down on the specific things they hate?

That’s Donald Trump’s signature move. Three days after Democrats flipped seven Senate seats—nine if you feel bullish on Mississippi and Alaska—and secured a House majority, winning 253 seats and counting, the president announced that his chief of staff, Susie Wiles, would be replaced by Stephen Miller.

“I am pleased to announce that Mr. Stephen Miller, the current Deputy Chief of Staff of the Trump Administration, and one of my most trusted advisors, will become the Chief of Staff starting Monday, November 9, 2026,” he wrote on Truth Social. “Susie Wiles, who has done an outstanding job, will be moving over to the Trump Political Operation. I want to thank her for her service as Chief of Staff — She has done a great job!”

Even though polls indicating a blue wave proliferated before the election, this particular outcome still comes as a surprise. After all, Wiles had apparently proven her ability to ride out storms before—including everything that rocked Trump’s 2024 campaign, which Wiles managed, and her choice to sit for 10 interviews with Vanity Fair’s Chris Whipple in 2025, during which she discussed Elon Musk’s drug use and JD Vance’s true motives for embracing Trump. “Despite his travails, Trump needs Wiles and almost everyone around him knows it,” Whipple recently wrote in a follow-up story tracing rumors of Wiles’s imminent axing.

But now Wiles’s luck has finally run out. Why? Perhaps because she is, in the end, a woman.

Trump’s first term was marked by all sorts of chaos, including frequent arrivals to and departures from the White House. (Remember Omarosa?) But it doesn’t take Jessica Fletcher to detect that this time around, there’s a distinct pattern when it comes to whom Trump is willing to toss aside when the going gets rough. The scalps of the no-scalps era all have barrel curls. As Texas representative Jasmine Crockett observed in April, “He will throw the incompetent women under the bus a lot faster than the incompetent men.”

On March 5, following an uprising against the administration’s mass-deportation effort that culminated in two protesters being killed by ICE agents in Minnesota, Secretary of Homeland Security Kristi Noem was axed. After wrath over the administration’s botched handling of the Epstein files, Attorney General Pam Bondi was fired in April.

Lori Chavez-DeRemer, Trump’s labor secretary, resigned later that month amid a misconduct investigation. Tulsi Gabbard, the director of national intelligence, who couldn’t sell Trump’s disastrous war with Iran, submitted her resignation in May, after her husband was diagnosed with cancer. Press secretary Karoline Leavitt stepped down—of her own accord—in August. Following an embarrassing Wall Street Journal investigation, Kimberly Guilfoyle, the ambassador to Greece—and former fiancée of Donald Trump Jr.—was forced to resign last month.

Though you may have already forgotten all about her, Katie Zacharia had perhaps the least distinguished tenure of all: She gave her first briefing as Trump’s new press secretary on a Tuesday and was forced out by Friday of the same week. Shortly before Wiles’s ouster this week, Harmeet Dhillon, who helped kill DEI after joining the civil rights division of Trump’s Justice Department, announced she was stepping down.

All this, and Pete Hegseth still has a job.

What are we to make of the fact that Trump’s departed all happen to harbor two X chromosomes? Could it be, perhaps, that President “Grab ’Em by the Pussy” has a problem with women—even the loyalists who are largely responsible for helping him claw his way back into the White House in the first place? (Does Trump win, or even run, in 2024 without Wiles? It’s a possibility I’d like to see Rodham author Curtis Sittenfeld explore in an upcoming novel.)

The one thing we can be sure about is that, as of right now, there are only three women left in Trump’s 21-person Cabinet. The members of that trio—Agriculture Secretary Brooke Rollins, Education Secretary Linda McMahon, and Kelly Loeffler, administrator of the Small Business Administration—might want to watch their blowout. (So should Natalie Harp.) Perhaps after they go, Trump will start employing the principles of diversity, equity, and inclusion while stabbing his allies in the back.

Thinking Machines debuts Inkling 2 Small amid reported $5B fundraising push

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Thinking Machines Labs Inc. has released a new large language model that can automate artificial intelligence training tasks.

The algorithm, Inkling 2 Small, made its debut late Thursday. Around the same time, Business Insider reported that Thinking Machine is in the process of raising a $5 billion funding round led by Accel. The company, which was founded last year by former OpenAI Group PBC Chief Technology Officer Mira Murati, is expected to receive a $40 billion valuation.

Thinking Machines trained Inkling 2 Small on a cluster of GB300 NVL72 racks. Each system contains 72 Blackwell Ultra graphics cards and half as many central processing units. The company completed the initial pre-training phase in 68 hours and subsequently refined the model through a nine-day reinforcement learning workflow. There were also a number of intermediate training steps.

Thinking Machines honed Inkling 2 Small’s programming capabilities by giving it hundreds of thousands of long-horizon coding challenges. The task collection included a number of particularly complicated “autoresearch” exercises. They required the model to post-train neural networks, optimize LLM components called kernels and perform other AI research work.

Thinking Machines put Inkling 2 Small’s machine learning skills to the test by asking it to fine-tune Qwen3.5-4B. The goal was to improve the open-source LLM’s ability to make predictions such as weather forecasts. According to the company, Inking 2 Small doubled the model’s forecasting score in 4.5 hours.

The LLM is also adapt at automating business tasks. It outperformed Spark Open, Kimi K3 and several other open-source models on three popular knowledge work benchmarks. Additionally, it earned higher scores across several financial task datasets.

Inkling 2 Small includes a vision encoder that can analyze images. Moreover, it enables the LLM to visually verify the quality of the graphics that it generates. If a user asks the model to create an e-commerce store, it can not only check the website’s code for bugs but also visually inspect product pages to check that they render correctly.

The vision encoder accounts for 292 million of Inking 2 Small’s 594 billion parameters. About 17 billion of those parameters activate when a user enters a prompt. Inking 2 Small can adjust the amount of computing power that it spends on tasks, which enables developers to trade off output quality for increased cost-efficiency or vice versa.

Particularly savvy users can extend the model’s capabilities by post-training, or customizing it, using their own data. Thinking Machines says that Inkling 2 Small learns faster than its earlier models. That should translate into more cost-efficient post-training runs.

Inkling 2 Small is available via Thinking Machines’ Tinker cloud service on launch. The company plans to release the model’s weights under an open-source license next week.

RealFi Nears Ethereum Launch, Weighs XRPL Expansion

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Home » Trendsetter News » RealFi Nears Ethereum Launch, Weighs XRPL Expansion

RealFi is targeting a December 1 Ethereum mainnet launch while exploring a possible XRP Ledger expansion.

RealFi plans to launch on Ethereum mainnet on December 1, according to a Cardano-focused host reviewing the project’s expansion plans. The Angry Crypto Show puts the move at the center of a broader question: whether Cardano-originated financial products can attract users and liquidity beyond their home network.

The host cites Token Terminal data showing RealFi has surpassed $121.7 million in asset transfer volume on Cardano. That figure measures transfers, not deposits or total value locked. The transcript does not specify the year of the planned Ethereum launch.

In remarks quoted from an interview with David Gokhshtein, RealFi CEO John O’Connor argues that stablecoins keep growing because they serve a practical purpose: moving stable-value money across borders. He identifies yield-bearing products as the fastest-growing part of that business.

An XRP Ledger expansion is less definite. O’Connor points to the network’s high fully diluted valuation and relatively limited DeFi activity as reasons to explore an integration.

“That’s a conversation we’re kicking off to see whether there’s value for us to come over there,” he says, according to the transcript. No launch date, partnership agreement or technical implementation is announced.

The host sees a potential exchange: Cardano-originated products could gain access to XRP users and liquidity, while offering additional financial functionality. That is the commentator’s investment thesis, not evidence of committed demand.

The video also reports that more than $450,000 worth of bitcoin had been supplied to Cardano’s Strike Finance within eight days of its integration of a bitcoin collateral asset. The figure offers another adoption signal, though the transcript provides no longer-term comparison.

Regulation occupies much of the discussion. Charles Hoskinson is quoted arguing that crypto must demonstrate uses beyond speculation and presenting Midnight’s privacy and identity capabilities as tools for operating in regulated markets. His political forecasts and assertions about ADA’s regulatory status remain opinions, not legal determinations.

Whether RealFi delivers its announced Ethereum launch, secures integrations and attracts sustained usage. An XRP Ledger expansion could broaden distribution, but O’Connor’s qualification matters – the project wants wider access only where the numbers justify it.

How to Moisturize Your Scalp in 6 Easy Steps

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Whenever my scalp feels tight and itchy and I notice white flakes falling, I wonder whether my love of hot showers (yes, I’m guilty of turning my small NYC shower into the equivalent of a hot spring) or my daily use of hot styling tools might be contributing. Whatever the cause, I know it’s time to give my scalp some extra attention.

As Hadley King, MD, a New York City board-certified dermatologist, explains, dry skin is generally characterized by water loss and a disrupted skin barrier. The same applies to the scalp, which Dr. King says needs both water-binding ingredients and barrier-supporting lipids to stay moisturized.

Several things can cause a dry scalp. Morgan Rabach, MD, a board-certified dermatologist and cofounder of LM Medical NYC, lists overwashing, using hot water in the shower, excessive hot tool use, and overprocessing hair with harsh chemicals as habits that may contribute. Certain skin conditions, like eczema or seborrheic dermatitis, and environmental factors such as cold weather or low humidity are also common causes.

Additionally, Y. Claire Chang, MD, a board-certified dermatologist at UnionDerm, adds that sebum production can decrease with age and during hormonal changes, making your scalp even more prone to dryness. These are all things you’ll want to address because, as we know, healthy hair comes from a healthy scalp. “The scalp is an extension of the skin on the face and body,” says Dr. Chang. “It is important to maintain a healthy skin barrier on the scalp just like the rest of the body.”

Luckily, it’s not too hard to keep your scalp moisturized. Just consider adding the following steps to your routine.

The first step to moisturizing your scalp is washing it with a gentle shampoo formulated with hydrating ingredients like glycerin, ceramides, and hyaluronic acid. Dr. King agrees and recommends using a gentle, non-medicated shampoo rather than a harsher clarifying shampoo if the scalp is dry. “The goal is to remove sweat, sebum, and product buildup without stripping the scalp’s protective lipids,” she says.

She says to look for gentle surfactants and recommends a shampoo like the Nutrire S.1 Calm Shampoo for Dry Scalp, which contains humectants like glycerin and panthenol to retain water and watermelon seed oil to provide emollient lipids that smooth dry skin. Alexis Stephens, DO, a board-certified dermatologist based in Coral Springs, Florida, likes the Vanicream Shampoo for Sensitive Skin as it is made without several of the most common irritants found in products, such as fragrance and essential oils. “It’s a good option for people with sensitive scalps or those who experience irritation from fragranced hair products,” Dr. Stephens says. “While it isn’t a moisturizer itself, it helps establish a gentler cleansing routine that supports scalp comfort.”

Like your face, your scalp skin can get dry when you overdo exfoliation. “A dry scalp generally doesn’t need aggressive scrubbing,” Dr. King says. “If someone has significant scale, it’s important to determine whether we’re dealing with seborrheic dermatitis or psoriasis rather than simply trying to exfoliate it away.”

After shampooing, Dr. Chang recommends using a moisturizing scalp treatment. Look for nourishing ingredients like hyaluronic acid, glycerin, or panthenol for that added moisture. You’ll want to part your hair into sections to apply the product directly to the scalp, she says, and she recommends something like the Kérastase Nutritive Hydrating Scalp Serum; it’s a lightweight leave-in treatment made with plant-based proteins, glycerin, panthenol, and niacinamide to provide targeted hydration. Dr. King likes The Ordinary Natural Moisturizing Factors + HA for Scalp, which she says is packed with moisturizing factors that replicate components naturally found in skin to help keep the skin hydrated.

Conditioning your hair is still an important part of your routine, particularly if you’re experiencing dryness along the hair shaft as well as on your scalp. Dr. Stephens says this is especially important for curly and coily hair textures, which may need more conditioning along the hair shaft even when the scalp itself isn’t dry. She likes the CeraVe Gentle Hydrating Conditioner, which contains known hydrating ingredients such as hyaluronic acid, niacinamide, and glycerin to support the skin barrier. “It’s also suitable for all curl patterns, including curly and coily hair, and helps leave the hair feeling softer and more manageable after cleansing,” she says.

Dr. Chang recommends applying conditioner primarily to the midlengths and ends to keep hair hydrated and to reduce breakage. We like the Nécessaire The Conditioner, as it’s fragrance-free and made with nourishing ingredients like glycerin, ceramides, and hyaluronic acid to keep hair hydrated and healthy.

Environmental stressors like the sun’s harmful UV rays can exacerbate dryness, which is why Dr. Chang encourages patients to use proper sun protection, such as hats and scalp-friendly sunscreen (we like the Sun Bum SPF 30 Sunscreen Scalp and Hair Mist Spray or the Coola Scalp Hair Spray Sunscreen SPF 30 Mist) on exposed areas. This is particularly important for anyone with thinning hair or a visible scalp.

Though it can feel soothing, Dr. Chang says to avoid washing your hair in excessively hot water, as it can dry out your scalp. Instead, use lukewarm water and keep your showers short to avoid stripping your scalp of its natural oils.

You’ll also want to avoid excessive product buildup, as heavy oils, dry shampoos, and styling products can accumulate on the scalp and potentially contribute to irritation. Regular cleansing will be key, so use the right water temperature.

Anyone can experience a dry scalp, regardless of skin tone, skin type, or hair texture, Dr. Stephens says. But people with sensitive skin, eczema, or a history of contact dermatitis may be more susceptible. Dr. Chang adds that lifestyle and environmental factors (think cold weather, low humidity, and indoor heating during the winter), as well as age, can play a role.

What Is the Difference Between Dry Scalp and Dry Hair?

Distinguishing between dry scalp and dry hair matters when you’re trying to find the best products to address each concern. “One of the biggest misconceptions I see, particularly among patients with curly and coily hair, is that dry hair automatically means dry scalp,” Dr. Stephens says. “It doesn’t. Tightly coiled hair is more prone to dryness along the hair shaft, partly because natural scalp oils don’t distribute as easily down the curls. But the scalp underneath may be perfectly balanced or even oily. That’s why I encourage people to think of their scalp and hair as two areas with different needs.”

Dr. Chang says to think of it like this: Hair dryness is about how moisture and natural oils travel along the hair shaft, while scalp dryness is more closely related to skin barrier function, sebum production, underlying skin conditions, and environmental exposures.

What Ingredients Should You Avoid to Prevent a Dry Scalp?

Dr. Rabach lists hair dyes, fragrance, and certain alcohols as common culprits that can dry out the scalp and may be worth avoiding. Dr. Stephens agrees and adds that harsh cleansing formulas with alcohol can strip away too much of your scalp’s protective surface (especially when used too frequently) and can be drying or irritating.

Other ingredients to look out for, she says, are essential oils. “They don’t necessarily dry out the scalp directly, but they can trigger irritation or allergic contact dermatitis, which may leave the scalp feeling tight, itchy, or uncomfortable,” she says.

With ingredients like salicylic acid, which help with scalp issues such as dandruff, you still want to be careful, as they may worsen dryness if you use them too often. She adds that just because something is labeled “sulfate-free,” it doesn’t automatically mean it's gentle. You want to consider the formulation as a whole. “It’s important to remember that the overall formulation matters more than any single ingredient,” she says.

What Is the Difference Between Dry Scalp and Dandruff?

Dr. Chang says flaking doesn’t always mean your scalp is dry. Dandruff is generally considered a mild form of seborrheic dermatitis, a condition associated with Malassezia yeast, scalp oils, and inflammation. You’ll need a special type of treatment for these scalp conditions, and it’s best to consult your dermatologist for a proper diagnosis.

Dr. King adds that if you’re experiencing persistent itching, redness, thick scales, crusting, significant flaking, or any pain, it may be time to seek professional help. “I’d recommend evaluation rather than assuming it’s simple dryness,” she says.

When we test and review a product, we take a holistic approach to deliver well-rounded product recommendations. First, we lean on Vogue’s vast network of experts—from board-certified dermatologists to celebrity estheticians—to gain professional insight into the industry’s standout products, ones these specialists would actually use on their clients. We pair their expertise with our editorial best practices to curate the thoughtful edits you read on our site.

For scalp moisturizing products, we selected the best based on ingredients, texture, ease of application, finish, and the hair-care concerns they address. GTA 6 PC may become a reality sooner than you think to avoid losing money to AI ‘slopcomps’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 not arriving on PC at the same time it launches on consoles is one of the biggest bummers in PC gaming history, but things may not be as bad as you fear if former Rockstar North director Obbe Vermeij’s predictions are correct.

It might be too far down the line for many to remember, but nearly two years passed between GTA 5‘s release on PlayStation 3 and Xbox 360 (yes, those were its original platforms), and its PC release. As Vermeij explains, and given that the richer part of humanity has fallen in incomprehensible love with the nonsense machine—and it’s finally starting to bite them in the butt—we might just be getting that GTA 6 PC port earlier than expected.

While the tech oligarchy spent their first years of investment in LLMs blissfully ignoring genAI’s need to feed on human creativity to (one day, hopefully) thrive, the industry is now facing hackers who have turned the plagiarism machine against its makers. In less than two months, people have been using AI tools to reverse engineer games never before seen on PC to achieve what they claim are ports that not only run very well, but also look much better than they did on the systems they were meant to run on. The biggest example, right now, is Bloodborne.

Sadly, even though Bloodborne is one of the most acclaimed games of all time, it has only ever been played by a relatively small number of people. It never got ported to PC or remastered to at least run at 60 fps because Sony likely never believed it would be worth the investment, and the company won’t be too bothered to see it running so well on PC a decade after its release.

Modern gamers have gotten used to a completely frictionless experience with a launcher featuring lots of games ready to play. Regardless of how well AI “jailbroken” games look and how well they might play, they still require people to learn and engage with a bunch of tools complicated enough to filter out most of the people who might’ve felt tempted to give it a shot. If you’re braver than most, please note that you won’t really be helping in any sort of valuable game preservation effort by getting the Bloodborne “PC port”, as the AI reverse engineering produces code that runs, but that’s completely unreadable, and thus useless for any community interested in engaging with and understanding the inner workings of the game.

GTA 6 is a whole different beast, the most hyped game in the history of the Industry, a media juggernaut that will certainly have millions of voracious PC gamers immediately engage with whatever tools promise them a free and even better-looking version of the GTA 6 they gotta pay a lot for to play on a console. They’re already turning it into a nightmare via DLSS5, so I’m pretty sure someone will try to bring it to the PC as soon as they can, likely disregarding all of Rockstar’s artistic intent along the way.

Either way, this could result in hefty losses for Rockstar, so we’re waiting to see how the company will face this new issue.

GTA 6 releases on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X|S on November 19.

The E.U. wants a new For You Page so that its citizens don’t get stuck in TikTok rabbit holes

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Does the European Union have the authority to mandate changes to TikTok‘s For You Page? The continental body is making that claim.

The E.U. announced on Wednesday that the addictive design of the FYP violates the Digital Services Act. A press release discusses the “rabbit hole effect” of TikTok’s infinitely scrolling vertical feed, which is filled with personalized recommendations.

“Every time a user scrolls or likes a video on TikTok, the algorithm serves more of the same leading users down rabbit holes they cannot easily escape from,” said Henna Virkkunen, the E.U.’s EVP of Tech Sovereignty, Security, and Democracy, in a statement. “The Digital Services Act makes platforms responsible for addressing the negative effects related to their recommender system. Today’s preliminary findings show again that TikTok needs to step up its efforts to protect users’ well-being, including those of minors.”

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

The E.U. passed the Digital Services Act in 2022 to give itself a mechanism it can use to regulate the world’s biggest social media companies. The DSA, as the law is known, is particularly focused on negative mental health consequences — including addictive behaviors — that can affect kids when they scroll through their feeds.

E.U. officials cited the DSA when they requested the development of a depersonalized TikTok algorithm, which the app subsequently rolled out. Now, as those leaders set their sights on the FYP, they are making similar requests. One point of contention is the “not interested” icon, which has long been cited as an ineffective form of control for users who don’t want to see specific types of content.

TikTok will get a chance to present documents and plead its case to the E.U. If, however, the app is ultimately found liable for its addictive feed, it could be compelled to pay up to 6% of its annual income in fines.

The validity of that fee structure could provide TikTok with its strongest defense. It previously joined forces with Meta to mount a legal challenge against the enforcement mechanism that undergirds the DSA, and a court ruled in its favor. The attempts to counter the DSA mirror Big Tech’s legal strategies in the United States, where TikTok is one of several companies fighting back against laws that accuse social media feeds of perpetrating psychological ills.

Though TikTok has pushed back against the DSA in the past, the E.U. is not backing down from its threats. TikTok has delivered a major boost to the European economy, but its activity in the region could wind up costing it billions.

Burger King reveals 2026 Advent calendar that lets fans build their own mini restaurant

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Burger King is bringing back its popular Advent calendar for the 2026 holiday season, this time allowing fans to build their own miniature version of the fast food chain’s restaurants.

The limited-edition collectible returns for its third consecutive year, featuring 12 days of surprises that gradually come together to create a miniature Burger King location.

The company announced the calendar on November 5, revealing that this year’s release will include miniature versions of popular menu items, restaurant fixtures, and holiday decorations.

Unlike traditional Advent calendars filled with chocolate or candy, Burger King’s 2026 version features 12 miniature collectibles that can be assembled into a complete restaurant.

Each door reveals a different piece of the restaurant, including a miniature Whopper in its updated box packaging, chicken nuggets, a soda fountain, and the King’s Booth.

The calendar also includes holiday decorations, such as a Christmas tree and reindeer, allowing fans to create a festive version of the restaurant.

On Day 12, the individual pieces come together to form a complete miniature Burger King restaurant that can be displayed on its own or added to a holiday village.

The final door also contains a miniature $5 Burger King digital coupon for Royal Perks members.

“We know Guests will be excited that for the third year in a row, the Advent Calendar is back, and it’s the best one ever,” said Joel Yashinsky, Chief Marketing Officer of Burger King US&C.

Burger King unveils bizarre new burger with popcorn as a topping

Burger King launches Hello Kitty kids meals with rare golden collectible toys

Burger King launches advent calendar with presents to celebrate 70-year history

“So this year, fans can build their very own BK restaurant, complete with a mini-Whopper®, nuggets and other items that make BK feel like BK.”

Burger King’s 2026 Advent calendar will cost $19.54, referencing the year the fast food chain was founded.

The collectible will be sold exclusively through BK’s website, with a limited number available each day.

Beginning Monday, November 16, Burger King will release just 110 calendars daily at 12 PM ET through Friday, November 20, while supplies last. That means only 550 calendars are scheduled to be available across the five-day release period.

According to Burger King, its previous two Advent calendars sold out in under five minutes, making this year’s limited release another opportunity for collectors to secure one of the holiday items.

The 2026 calendar will only be available online, rather than at participating Burger King restaurants.

North of North, the Canadian comedy set in the Arctic Prince of Wales Island, is back on Netflix for season 2. If you’re catching up or in need of a crash course, here’s your guide to the cast & characters in North of North season 2.

The show is about an Inuk woman named Siaja who blows up her life in search of something more nontraditional in terms of gender and sexuality. That’s not easy to do in a small town where her ex-husband was the golden boy. Siaja’s ever-evolving family also shakes things up, especially in season 2 when she meets her half-sister, Olivia, played by Kesha Allurut. As for the cast and characters who’ve been on this journey since season one, here’s who you need to know.

Siaja is a young mother who quits her job and gets a divorce.

Anna Lambe’s previous television credits include the fourth season of True Detective, Three Pines, Alaska Daily, Trickster, and Diggtown. Livery of the Week: JetBlue’s Florida Panthers Design

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Editor’s Note: AirlineGeeks is proud to present our ‘Livery of the Week’ series. Every Friday, a team member will share an airline livery, which can be from the past, present, or even a special scheme. Some airline liveries are works of art. The complexity associated with painting around critical flight components and the added weight requires outside-the-box thinking from designers. The average airliner can cost upwards of $200,000 to repaint, creating a separate aircraft repainting industry as a result.

Have an idea for a livery that we should highlight? Drop us a line.

Since the beginning of its operations in 2000, New York-based airline JetBlue has always considered South Florida as one of its primary markets, with a presence at Fort Lauderdale-Hollywood Airport that has grown during the past 25 years to include up to 25 aircraft based overnight at the airport and over 2,000 people supporting its operations in Broward County.

As a sign of its commitment to the South Florida community, JetBlue has announced a multi-year extension for an expanded partnership with the Florida Panthers, the NHL’s southernmost franchise that captured back-to-back Stanley Cups in 2023-24 and 2024-25. The partnership will see JetBlue present a newly redesigned Champions Club at the Amerant Bank Arena in Sunrise, Florida, home of the Panthers, and to celebrate the announcement, the airline unveiled a special Panthers-themed livery for one of its Airbus A320 aircraft.

The new livery draws its inspiration from the Panthers’ jersey, therefore sporting the iconic Panthers’ red across the entire fuselage, with the outline of a golden panther all across the central part of the fuselage, right next to JetBlue’s logo. The Panthers’ shield logo is on the aircraft tail in navy and gold.

Bold red letters spelling FLORIDA appear on the bottom of the aircraft, where there’s also a leaping cat, the team’s logo. More symbols of the Panthers and the State of Florida appear in other parts of the fuselage: a simplified Florida flag appears near the front of the aircraft together with a palm tree and a hockey stick, which represent the secondary logo for the team.

“South Florida has been part of JetBlue’s story since our very first flight more than two decades ago, and we’re continuing to build on that history in a big way as Broward County’s largest airline,” said Marty St. George, JetBlue’s president, in a press release. “The Panthers know a thing or two about winning in South Florida, and we’re bringing that same energy as we expand our network, elevate the customer experience and deepen our commitment to the community. ‘Go Cats Go’ is a fun way to celebrate our shared passion for South Florida and team up with a franchise that knows how to make some noise.”

The aircraft chosen to display this special livery is an Airbus A320, registered as N784JB, delivered to the airline on Feb. 11, 2011.

The original name of the aircraft was “Blue And Infinity And Beyond,” which has been changed to “Go Cats Go” following the repainting in this new livery.
